Spalletti: ‘When Juventus win, people act like opponents are weak or ridiculous’

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Luciano Spalletti took the opportunity to stand up for Juventus after a 2-1 Europa League victory at AZ Alkmaar. ‘When we win, people act like our opponents are weak or ridiculous, but we showed courage.’

It was far from an easy task for the Bianconeri, as AZ Alkmaar were unbeaten at home in 17 European fixtures going back to October 2023, and the Old Lady was decimated by injuries.

They surged into a 2-0 lead with Randal Kolo Muani providing the assist for Teun Koopmeiners, facing his hometown club, and then the ex-PSG striker scored the second himself when combining with Nick Woltemade.

It was a very tense finale, as AZ Alkmaar fought back with the Mexx Meerdink tap-in after Lloyd Kelly was robbed on the touchline, then Juve rode their luck as Meerdink was offside for a potential equaliser in added time.

“I am very satisfied, because we played well against a very strong team,” Spalletti told Sky Sport Italia.

“I remember when all of you laughed in my face when we beat NEC 5-0, you acted as if they were a ridiculous team, but NEC now have seven points in the Europa League.

“So perhaps people need to re-evaluate how they consider us, humility, and giving credit to a team without just constantly wanting to put pressure on.

“When we win a game, people act as if it was because the opponents were weak, or finding some excuses. We played a strong game against a great team tonight, and as we saw the moment our intensity dropped, they will take advantage.

“We’ve seen in the Champions League, Europa League, these sides might have a predictable way of doing things, but it becomes a strength when they can do it that many times. It is constantly hammering away at those situations, we were pinned back, worried about whether or not to push out from defence, and a through ball caused a goal.

“There were mad scrambles, if the game lasted for another three or four minutes, I don’t think we would’ve taken the result home.”

Considering Juventus were so in control for 70-odd minutes, Spalletti sees the need for insurance rather than learning how to defend a lead better.

“What we needed to do was score a third goal. The way we played, we had to score a third goal there. When you win away to a team that hasn’t lost at home in 17 European games, winning 14 of them, that is a sign you played with authority and quality. We showed the right courage.”

Koopmeiners and Kolo Muani had arguably their best performances of the season so far in the Juventus jersey, yet Spalletti wanted more.

“Kolo had a few situations when he had the chance to get running and become unstoppable, but we never managed to give him the right pass to get into that space. So we had to suffer on the edge of the area, we did it well too.

“Those who came off the bench could really do damage on the counter with Boga and Ekhator, instead we got pinned back and felt anxious, nobody was sure whether to come or go, and got stuck in the middle.”

This performance does build on Sunday’s 2-0 Serie A victory against Napoli and suggest Juventus might be on the right track, while they are certainly flying high in the Europa League with three wins and a 2-2 draw against Rennes.

“Some like Lucumi did the unseen dirty work, he often had to deal with the ball on his right foot, it was not his position and he did it well. Kalulu plays almost non-stop, the poor thing was about to ask for a substitution at the end as he was exhausted.

“There were many positive signs today, but the most important was that we went into their half, accepted being man to man for a long time.”

Spalletti was impressed by AZ Alkmaar goalkeeper Jari De Busser and feels Italian teams can learn from this Dutch approach.

“In Italy so many said a goalkeeper just has to make saves, but when he can play with his feet like this, he annuls all the pressing that the opponents do, he neutralises any situation that you prepare, and he is the extra man. The goalkeeper makes the difference. Did you see the pass he made to spark that move?

“The fact we came here and accepted that duel, pushing into their half and accepting the challenge, was the biggest victory. I also liked the attitude from those on the bench, who shouted and experienced it as if they were on the field.”

Now Juventus must prepare for their final match before the break for international duty, a tricky Serie A trip to old rivals Fiorentina on Sunday evening.

“My concern is the way we conceded the goal, with everyone waiting to see who would take it, allowing the others to move around. I continue to say a decision that is not made is the worst possible kind, even more so than a bad decision.

“Being hesitant, waiting for the situation to develop, that is what I do not want to see.”

American Bitcoin’s $126M profit masks mining pressure as rivals turn to AI

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The Eric Trump-backed miner reported $126 million in quarterly net income, largely driven by gains on its Bitcoin holdings, despite rising mining costs and shrinking margins.

American Bitcoin reported a quarterly net income of $126 million on Wednesday, but rising mining costs and falling profits from mining raise questions about its long-term strategy as major rivals pivot towards artificial intelligence.

The Eric Trump-backed company’s profit was driven by a $197.3 million noncash gain on its Bitcoin (BTC) holdings. Meanwhile, mining revenue fell 5.5% to $63.4 million from the previous quarter, while the cost to mine one BTC rose about 15% to $42,100.

Excluding gains from its Bitcoin holdings, American Bitcoin would have reported an operating loss of approximately $5.5 million, according to its financial statements. The company also recorded a separate $64.3 million loss on derivatives.

“Despite seasonal power cost pressures, we continued mining Bitcoin at a structural discount to spot,” American Bitcoin CEO Mike Ho said in Wednesday’s earnings announcement.

Earnings per share stood at $1.73, beating analysts’ consensus estimate of $0.26, according to Yahoo Finance data.

Bitcoin was trading at $76,119, down 5% over the past 24 hours as of 8:50 p.m. UTC, according to CoinGecko data. Shares of American Bitcoin (ABTC), meanwhile, closed Thursday down about 15% at $5.31.

The results highlight an interesting divide among Bitcoin miners: while American Bitcoin continues mining and holding BTC, competitors like Riot Platforms, MARA Holdings and Core Scientific are investing in AI to find new sources of revenue.

David Gamble, founder of asset management company Enigma Holdings, which focuses on Bitcoin mining and AI infrastructure, said American Bitcoin’s reported profit does not tell the full story of its mining business.

“Ultimately, mining Bitcoin below its market price doesn’t automatically mean you’re creating shareholder value,” Gamble told Cointelegraph.

American Bitcoin’s gross mining profit fell to $25.8 million from $33 million in the previous quarter. Its reported mining margin dropped to 41% from 49%, leaving the company with a smaller share of mining revenue after direct costs.

The company’s $63.4 million in quarterly revenue also fell short of analysts’ estimates of $74.35 million, according to Yahoo Finance data, a gap of nearly 15%.

The company mined 891 BTC during the quarter, down from 932 BTC in the previous three months. It also said higher electricity prices were the main reason mining costs increased.

“I don’t necessarily think their accumulation strategy is flawed, but I do think it raises questions about long-term sustainability,” Gamble said, pointing to increasing mining difficulty and the need to keep investing in newer and more efficient technology to compete.

Compared with the same period last year, revenue was nearly unchanged, falling from $64.2 million to $63.4 million. Reported profit, however, jumped from $3.5 million to $126 million, largely reflecting gains on the company’s Bitcoin holdings.

Related: Bitcoin miners face widening AI financing costs as lenders scrutinize deals

American Bitcoin’s strategy comes as competitors like Riot Platforms, MARA Holdings and Core Scientific rapidly expand into AI.

Riot is building large AI data centers, according to its latest earnings. Meanwhile MARA is expanding into AI while continuing to mine Bitcoin. Core Scientific, on the other hand, has already shifted much of its business toward AI data centers.

American Bitcoin, by contrast, continues building its BTC reserves, holding 8,427 BTC at the end of September, up from 3,418 BTC a year earlier.

“The real competitive advantage in this industry is shifting toward energy infrastructure,” Gamble said. “It’s no longer just about how much Bitcoin you can mine or how much hashrate you can deploy.”

Gamble also explained that Bitcoin miners and AI companies are competing for the same power supply, and AI data centers could make more money than Bitcoin mining in some areas.

“That doesn’t mean every mining facility can simply transition into AI,” he said. “You still have to account for depreciation, equipment replacement, financing costs, and the opportunity cost of deploying that capital elsewhere.”

Newsom Commutes All 548 Death Sentences in California: Can't 'Morally Kill'

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

California Gov. Gavin Newsom commuted all state death sentences for 548 death row prisoners, explaining in an open letter to Californians that he firmly believes “state-sanctioned killing is incompatible” with state values.

In the letter announcing the move on Thursday, November 5, Newsom wrote that he’s using his authority as governor to commute the death sentences to life without the possibility of parole.

“Many Californians feel that those who commit brutal crimes deserve to die. But as Governor, I have concluded the state cannot morally kill them,” the letter read. “I do, however, pray that this action will finally separate the memories of the victims of these crimes from the fate of those who harmed them.”

“We can now let these offenders die in obscurity while we forever remember and honor their victims,” Newsom added.

While the governor has the authority to commute sentences for prisoners convicted of a single felony, California Supreme Court approval is needed for commutations of death row inmates convicted of multiple felonies, according to the governor’s office.

Of those commuted by Newsom, the state supreme court needs to approve clemency for 382 people convicted of more than one felony, per the Associated Press, which cited numbers from Newsom’s office.

“I acknowledge that my decision to exercise my constitutional clemency authority requires the Justices to participate in a weighty decision they did not choose to take up and with which they may personally disagree,” Newsom’s letter stated. “I profoundly appreciate the integrity and expertise the Court brings to the task of clemency review.”

Newsom’s decision is considered the “largest mass clemency” in the U.S., according to the Death Penalty Information Center.

It comes after Newsom issued a moratorium on executions after becoming California’s governor in March 2019. There hasn’t been a death row execution in the state since 2006.

“The decision is among the most difficult in my career, not because I doubt its necessity for our state but because I know its announcement may surface excruciating memories for victims’ families,” Newsom’s letter stated. “Today I am acting on my long and deeply held conviction that individually and collectively we are stronger as a society when we reject state-sanctioned killing.”

Newsom issued the commutations ahead of a potential presidential run, according to the Associated Press.

Infamous California death row inmates include Richard Allen Davis, who was convicted in the 1993 kidnapping and killing of 12-year-old Polly Klaas, the outlet reported.

In a Thursday statement shared in a news release issued by the U.S. Campaign to End the Death Penalty, Klaas’ sister, Jess Nichol, said, “I won’t pretend I’ve always opposed the death penalty. I used to think the man who killed my sister deserved execution.”

“But over time I’ve realized that whether he’s executed has absolutely nothing to do with my recovery or with real public safety,” Nichol added. “The system tells survivors that execution matters to our healing. It doesn’t. It’s a distraction from what we actually need.”

Newsom wrote in his letter that he acknowledges his clemency decision “will anger and hurt some Californians.”

Texas Man Admits to $1.5 Million Deed Fraud Scheme Involving Properties in 4 States

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

An empty piece of land might seem like an easy real estate investment opportunity, but for unsuspecting property owners across the country, those plots of land became targets in an alleged $1.5 million deed fraud scheme carried out by four men posing as the landowners.

Moshe Levi, 61, of Carrollton, TX, has admitted to helping carry out the plan, which involved impersonating property owners, arranging fraudulent sales, and laundering the proceeds, federal prosecutors announced this week.

On Wednesday, Levi pleaded guilty in a Boston federal courtroom to conspiracy to commit wire fraud and conspiracy to commit money laundering. The fraudulent property sales totaled more than $1.5 million, according to prosecutors.

Levi was one of three people indicted in July 2026. According to court filings, Levi and his alleged co-conspirators targeted vacant, unencumbered properties in Massachusetts, Georgia, Indiana, and Tennessee.

“Mr. Levi deeply regrets his conduct,” Levi's attorney, Ian Gold, tells Realtor.com®. “The plea represents an important step toward accountability to the victims and society.”

The scheme hit home for Halla Shami Jaraki, who owns a vacant lot on Mattison Drive in Concord, MA, but lives in South Carolina with her husband, Omar.

The couple had hoped to return to Massachusetts or pass the land along to one of their sons. Instead, their property was sold in 2024 for roughly $525,000 by someone allegedly posing as the owner, according to The Concord Bridge.

Developers began to build on the property but were stopped when the couple obtained an injunction against them. According to prosecutors, this couple’s experience was how the scheme worked.

Levi and his co-conspirators targeted vacant properties owned by people who lived out of state, then created email and internet-based phone accounts in the owners' names. They also obtained fake driver’s licenses and passports with the names of the property owners.

The group then used the forged documents and accounts to impersonate the owners of the properties and then allegedly persuaded real estate agents to list the properties, negotiate the sale of the properties, and transfer the deed to the unsuspecting buyer for payment.

The scheme allegedly extended beyond the fraudulent sales. Prosecutors said Levi and his co-conspirators moved money through individual and corporate bank accounts they controlled, using checks and wire transfers to conceal the source, ownership, and control of the proceeds.

Since around June 2023, Levi and others have shared or attempted to share in approximately $1 million in illegal proceeds, prosecutors said.

In April 2025, the FBI’s Boston office warned of an increase in deed fraud cases, with the rise of remote real estate transactions providing opportunities for “title pirates.” Property owners are being urged to be on the lookout for a number of warning signs, including missing property tax and water bills and sudden and unexplained increases in utility charges at vacant properties.

In a survey released in September from the American Land Title Association, 59% of title firms reported at least one incident of seller impersonation fraud in the prior year, which is more than double the 28% reported in the 2024 survey.

Levi will be sentenced on Feb. 11, 2027. He faces up to 20 years on each of the two counts of conspiracy to commit wire fraud.

Prosecutors said they would recommend prison time “at the low end” of the sentencing guidelines, along with a fine “at the low end” of the guidelines, three years of supervised release, forfeiture, and at least $1.34 million in restitution.

Levi was charged along with Kyon James of Middleborough, MA, and Bradley Beauge of Somerset, NJ. Those charges for money laundering conspiracy are currently pending.

A fourth co-conspirator is based in Nigeria, but prosecutors have not publicly released their name.

Airbnb says it’s launching an AI travel agent in the first half of 2027

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Airbnb has teased its plans to build its own AI travel agent before, but on Thursday the company announced that the new AI feature will launch in the first half of 2027. In the company’s Q3 shareholder letter on Thursday, Airbnb also provided more detail about how the agent will work, what it will actually do, and how it’s different from a typical AI chatbot.

In prior interviews, CEO Brian Chesky sketched out the travel company’s approach to AI, describing a plan to build an AI agent that has a richer interface than a conventional chatbot. He also expressed his opinions about how chatbots aren’t the right interface for travel, noting that people take pleasure in the planning process and in anticipating a trip. Any agent that it built, then, shouldn’t take away from the inspiration and the browsing, he told TechCrunch in an interview last month.

Chesky today reiterated that belief, telling analysts on the earnings call that people won’t use one AI agent for everything.

“Today’s AI agents like Muse and Instinct are good at simple routine tasks like making a restaurant reservation or scheduling an appointment. But planning a trip is different,” he said. “Travel is aspirational. Discovering where to go and where to stay is part of the experience. In fact, 80% of travelers say they enjoy spending time researching trips. People want to look at photos. They want to compare homes. They want to make decisions with people they’re traveling with,” he continued. “And I don’t think the general-purpose agents are the best to do that. So that is why we are building our own AI agent,” Chesky said.

Airbnb described its agent as having a personalized interface that’s designed specifically for travel, where it will “build an interface” around a user’s question instead of simply responding as a chatbot would.

“What we are doing is developing a custom-designed AI-native experience that responds to what guests are looking for,” Chesky said. “So instead of relying on traditional chat alone, which is very text-heavy—which, again, is definitely not the way you should plan travel, our agent draws from a library of hundreds of travel-specific interface components—maps, photos, comparisons, calendars, itineraries—and composes them in real time to match what they’re looking for.”

The exec said the agent will feel “a little bit like generative UI” as it’s designed to offer up “the right interface at the right time” for someone’s specific travel needs.

In its shareholder letter, the company touted that its nearly 20 years of experience in bringing hosts and guests together is informing how the agent is built, as it incorporates insights about how people both book and host on its platform. The agent will also be able to help travelers beyond the booking itself.

“Our agent will be able to help guests throughout their trip—from changing a reservation or resolving a payment issue to getting help with refunds, rebooking, or 24-hour safety support. These are things an outside agent can’t easily do on its own,” the company wrote.

Over time, Airbnb expects the agent will be able to help travelers with other aspects of their trip, including not only homes and hotels, but also services and experiences. Services and experiences are two areas that have become a bigger focus for the company following its redesign last year.

While the company previously said to expect its AI agent sometime in the next year, it’s now more specifically committing to roll out the new technology to consumers in the first half of 2027.

The news of the agent’s release was delivered alongside strong third-quarter earnings, with revenue growing 19% to $4.87 billion, topping Wall Street estimates. It also raised its 2026 revenue growth forecast, which it now predicts will be in the high teens, instead of the mid-teens.

“Airbnb is back in growth mode,” Chesky told analysts on the Q3 earnings call on Thursday. “It’s rare for a marketplace of our size to reaccelerate growth, but that’s exactly what we’re doing. Q3 was our fastest revenue growth in more than three years,” he added.

Everything you need to know about WoW Forever leveling

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Grinding to Level 60 in WoW: Forever will feel painfully familiar, but Blizzard hopes it's not as painful as Classic's.

World of Warcraft: Forever welcomes everyone to Azeroth for the first time for, well, the third time. And anyone who’s built a career out of creating alts throughout Retail and in Classic would want to just go through the motions and get to Level 60 ASAP to enjoy all the new endgame features WoW: Forever has to offer.

But with Level 60 as the capstone, it’s not going to be as quick a ride compared to other WoW expansions. And if this is your first time dipping your toes into an intense MMO like WoW, here’s a quick lesson in leveling in Forever.

For an MMO that lets characters experience the highs of being Level 120 (back in Battle for Azeroth, at least), Level 60 for Forever feels awfully fast. Or the worse alternative, the mid-point grind will probably be horrifyingly slow.

“We did not change the experience curve,” Kris Zierhut, WoW: Forever‘s principal designer told Sean Keach of The Sun. “So what we’ve really done is just created a different path. The experience curve remains the same.”

Blizzard devs also highlighted this with similar metrics from the WoW: Forever Beta. The average leveling time from Level 1 to 20 in Classic is 20.4 hours, compared to 20.5 in the Forever Beta.

“The big intent is to get you into the world and keep you in it,” Josh Greenfield, WoW: Forever senior game designer, said in the WoW: Forever podcast. “Part of the game is to protect and nurture social experiences. We want to keep you in the world as much as possible.”

But what does this mean for the rest of the leveling experience?

Leveling in WoW: Forever is standard-fare RPG mechanics. You get XP by turning in quests, killing mobs, and discovering new areas. For monsters specifically, you get more XP by fighting higher-level mobs compared to lower-level ones.

From your starting zone, expect that quests will eventually get you to a nearby zone with mobs scaled to your general level.

Expect to encounter more lower-level mobs in starting zones to help get you up to speed. Then they get less and less the more you level up. Blizzard said they’ll squeeze in extra spawn rates during launch and will balance things out eventually.

Leveling can get confusing when other people get involved. If you’re in a high-population area, chances are there are a lot more people also farming the same mobs. This is where tagging comes in.

Basically, if you tag or attack the monster first, you get first dibs on the XP. The monster’s name turns gray for them, which means someone else is already actively engaging the monster.

Even if they kill the monster, you get the XP. The same works if you kill a mob someone already tagged; this time they get the XP instead of you.

If this seems too confusing, you can always just buddy up.

No adventurer is an island, and one of the M’s in MMORPG does stand for “multiplayer,” so WoW: Forever does incentivize group play. When you party up with other players, you share any XP you get from killing mobs.

At first glance, this seems counterintuitive since you can just earn more if you kill them solo. But the idea is that you can theoretically tackle more powerful mobs and earn even more XP just because you’re in a team.

Plus, you don’t share Quest XP, so it helps to tackle zones together as a unit.

Alternatively, instead of buddying up, just take turns getting first tag on a monster. Everyone else helps, but the tagger gets the XP. Then rotate.

But take note that the higher-level the “helper,” the less XP the tagger will get.

Professions are leveled separately. Basically, things you do that actively involve the Profession will earn XP for it. After reaching a certain level, you have to rank up your Profession via the Trainer in order to access more benefits and potential craftable items.

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

One of the fastest ways to tackle quests in WoW: Forever is the ages-old strategy of doing it zone by zone. Blizzard built Azeroth in such a way that quests will have you naturally go from one area to the next until such a point that you can choose whichever common-leveled zones you want to spend time in.

With an add-on like Questie, you can have a rough estimate of the claimable quests in the zone you’re in and gauge your plan from there. The usual strategy is to accept all quests in one zone, do everything in the vicinity, then turn in everything at once. By the time a quest sends you to the next zone, that’s the sign it’s time to move on.

When you stay in a safe location like an inn, a city, or even with the Cozy Sleeping Bag, you’ll end up in something called a rested state. In here, you start to accumulate something called Rested XP.

This is a concept taken straight from Classic. You know you’re there when your XP bar becomes blue instead of purple. As long as you’re eligible for Rested XP, you gain double the XP from monster kills. This eligibility depends on a “stockpile” of XP you accumulate as you stay rested. Here are other things to consider:

Always set your Hearthstone in an inn. If you have to log out at any point, you’ll have accumulated some Rested XP by the time you come back.

Since Forever is in part a reimagining of the vanilla WoW launch, it’s no surprise vets will have a specific leveling approach in mind. It’s a ritual at that point, especially for those who have an alt for just about any situation.

But take note that part of Blizzard’s plan is to focus less on farming mobs and doing more quests.

“Moving a lot of that experience (leveling) off of just the kills and onto the quests really incentivizes you to do that (the quests) at least once,” Greenfield said on the WoW: Forever podcast.

This also goes for dungeons, where dungeon quests now give more XP while dungeon mobs give less. Prioritize clearing dungeon quests for the first time, as this is where the most XP gain happens.

Anyone who’s ever tried leveling a character in Classic will be intimately familiar with the dreaded Level 40 grind. It’s just a point in anyone’s adventuring career where quests are scarce, and there’s really no “logical” point of zoning progression.

Back in the day, your choices beyond the questlines would be bouncing to and from your preferred farming zones. Forever wants to solve this by adding a couple more regions (and theoretically, quests) to help with that grind:

These zones will be new additions to staple zones of this level, such as:

Logging into World of Warcraft: Forever to see a Level 60 cap might make it feel… lacking for some reason. Level 60 always felt like the midpoint to something else, not really a capstone digit.

It could have been the history. WoW: Forever is pegged as a reimagining of the 2004 Vanilla launch, which did have the Level 60 cap.

But then again, it only took two years before The Burning Crusade raised that to Level 70. Come 11 years later, Battle for Azeroth gave us a monumental peak of Level 120, then Shadowlands squished everything back to Level 60. Then Blizzard just couldn’t get its hands off the max level, so here we are at Midnight, a couple more expansions later, with a Level 90 cap.

Basically, it felt un-Blizzard for them to declare WoW: Forever to be strictly until Level 60. If they keep the promise, leveling will be more interesting if they follow through by introducing more routes for that midpoint grind. And if they somehow start pushing the meter again, none of us would be really surprised.

World of Warcraft: Forever is out right now for PC and macOS.

Scott Colom’s Mississippi Votes Are Still Rolling In—and So Are the Views on His Viral Campaign Ads

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

In an era when short-form-video political messaging reigns supreme, it might be a tiebreaker, and even kingmaker, in Mississippi’s Senate midterm race, where Democratic challenger Scott Colom narrowly leads incumbent Republican senator Cindy Hyde-Smith as ballots continue to be counted on Thursday, November 5.

On October 13, Colom’s campaign released an ad that opens with a clip from a televised February interview featuring Hyde-Smith, a cattle farmer and former agriculture commissioner, responding to a question about rising beef prices by offering that Mississippians “have so many proteins to choose from.” The ad then cuts to a man grilling burgers. “Pick another protein?” he asks. “Sheee-it. I’d rather pick another senator instead.” The ad appears to have been made by content creator Ryan Sayles. Vanity Fair has reached out to Sayles as well as Colom’s campaign for comment.

The message, which highlighted the Colom campaign’s commitment to cost cutting, capitalized on Hyde-Smith’s gaffe to the tune of more than 2.8 million views between X (formerly Twitter), Instagram, and Facebook within the first 36 hours, according to the Clarion-Ledger. Former president Barack Obama’s video endorsement emphasizing Colom’s “Christian faith,” released just a day earlier, earned just over 700,000 views on the platforms in nearly double the time.

Colom currently has the edge in the historically deep-red state: If elected, he would become the first Democratic Mississippi Senator in more than 40 years—as part of the blue wave that swept the 2026 midterms, in which Democrats seized back control of both the House and Senate from MAGA-era Republicans. In Mississippi, which President Trump won by roughly 20 points in 2024, Hyde-Smith was favored to win. That is, until mid-October, when one of Colom’s campaign ads went viral, turning the Southern enclave into a surprise battleground state.

The day before Halloween, Colom made waves when he paid tribute to the Oscar-winning Ryan Coogler film Sinners, casting himself and his twin brother, Andrew, as fictional twins Smoke and Stack Moore, both portrayed by Michael B. Jordan in the movie. The film, which was set in the 1932 Mississippi Delta, follows the Black brothers as they try to keep white vampires away from their juke joint. The ad, titled “Sinators,” and released by Colom on October 30 with the disclaimer that “this is a joke!” shows the two men guarding a door as a blonde woman, who seems to be a proxy for Hyde-Smith, tries to enter. Colom says the duo have to “hold her off until the sun rises on Election Day” in the ad, which garnered more than 380,000 views on X alone prior to the November 3 election, as reported by the Sun Herald. If Colom wins, he would be Mississippi’s first Black senator since the Reconstruction era (1865–1877). VF has reached out to Coogler for comment on Colom’s campaign ad.

Colom’s attention-grabbing content hasn’t come without controversy. During the campaign, the candidate was compelled to delete another ad that featured an artificial-intelligence-generated depiction of Hyde-Smith running away from reporters after the state attorney general Lynn Fitch threatened legal action against him. (Fitch, a Republican, helped support Texas’s lawsuit challenging the 2020 election results, and was also counsel for the petitioner on Dobbs v. Jackson Women’s Health Organization, the case which ultimately overturned Roe v. Wade.)

Shortly before the release of the Sinners ad, but after the AI dustup, the Senate Majority PAC, the flagship super PAC for Democratic Senate candidates aligned with Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer, announced it would spend $1.2 million on a broadcast TV and digital ad campaign for Colom. “It is clear Colom is running a competitive race that has put Hyde-Smith on her heels,” Senate Majority PAC spokesperson Lauren French said in a statement to The Hill.

Colom, a local prosecutor, and Hyde-Smith, seeking her second full term in the Senate, had a past connection heading into this political stand-off. Hyde-Smith blocked Colom’s nomination for federal judge in 2023, citing his support for transgender rights and his having previously been boosted by a George Soros–linked PAC, according to the Mississippi Free Press. Colom told ABC News this “has nothing to do with the run for me…. As a Christian, I forgive her. It’s about the people in Mississippi.”

Since Mississippi elections do not have an automatic recount process, the opponents face a potential legal challenge over the results of their Senate race, in which Colom holds a razor-thin lead over Hyde-Smith. Colom later echoed a similar sentiment. “It’s not about me. It’s about the people in Mississippi,” the smiling Colom told reporters while speaking to constituents at a local establishment on Thursday morning. “They decided to go in a new direction.”

Feds warn grocers: Don't chill on cyber defenses as freezer hacking spree heats up

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

No documented attacks against American supermarkets…yet

If you have any industrial smart freezers, look out. The US federal government is urging owners and operators to disconnect them from the internet, apply patches, change default passwords, and take other security measures to prevent the kind of attacks that have become prevalent in Europe.

“We are seeing these systems targeted across critical infrastructure sectors globally, putting inventory and equipment at risk,” FBI Cyber Branch Operations Director Brett Leatherman said in a statement.

Since mid-October, unknown miscreants have hacked thousands of remote-controlled refrigeration systems across hundreds of grocery stores in France, Belgium, Ireland, Luxembourg, and Sweden. The attacks increase the temperature inside fridges to highs of 50ºC (122°F), spoiling food, and sparking fires.

The Register asked the FBI if it has documented any successful or attempted attacks against refrigeration and cold chain monitoring systems in America. We had not received a response to our questions at the time of publication, nor are we aware of any documented digital attacks against these systems in American supermarkets.

But there are signs that stateside operators are aware of the issue.

Dutch grocery giant Ahold Delhaize, which operates US supermarket chains Food Lion, Giant Food, and Stop & Shop, last month said it proactively took IT systems offline following a cyberattack that affected most of its US and European brands.

Delhaize supermarkets in Europe are among those hit by the destructive freezer attacks.

The Pentagon is also reportedly treating a series of freezer malfunctions that spoiled food at military bases across the US in August as a suspected cyberattack, according to Reuters, which cited four officials familiar with the matter.

In a Thursday advisory, the US FBI, Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA), Health and Human Services, and departments of Agriculture and Transportation warned organizations about the ongoing cyber threats facing operators of industrial refrigeration and cold-chain monitoring systems.

“The actors may exploit vulnerabilities or weaknesses in unpatched or internet-accessible controllers to remotely alter settings and force units into continuous heating and defrost cycles, which can cause widespread inventory loss and equipment hazards,” the agencies said.

Attackers exploit these flaws by bypassing authentication, injecting arbitrary code, and abusing weak configurations, according to the feds. Once criminals gain access to the systems, they can change the temperature to spoil food or temperature-controlled medications, start fires, and ultimately harm people who consume products that haven’t been stored at proper temperatures.

To prevent these scenarios, the government organizations urge owners and operators to “immediately” implement best practices and security protections. First and foremost on the list: do not expose industrial control systems and devices to the public internet.

Iranian hackers used internet-accessible programmable logic controllers (PLCs) to break into more than 100 US water systems in July. This sort of exposed device, which often lacks password protection, is a boon to miscreants looking for an easy button to gain access to critical facilities.

“Should remote access be necessary, protect it by using a firewall or edge device and updating it to the latest version to reduce security vulnerabilities,” the feds advise.

Create a dedicated VLAN for operational technology (OT) devices, to isolate industrial devices from business networks.

Replace default passwords with strong passwords and policies, turning on phishing-resistant multifactor authentication (MFA) whenever possible.

Apply patches in a timely manner, and retire and replace legacy devices that no longer receive security updates from the manufacturer.

Define and maintain a device and IP allow-list, and limit access to OT devices to only those on the list.

Maintain and test backups. These should be kept offsite and offline.

And finally: enable logging and regularly review logs for any unusual activity.®

Teamsters Sue Horizon Air Over Pilot Rest Rules

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The Teamsters have sued Horizon Air, alleging the Alaska Air Group subsidiary violated federal labor law by changing how it handles pilots who report fatigue.

The Teamsters Airline Division and Local 1224 filed the lawsuit Oct. 23 in the U.S. District Court for the Western District of Washington. The union alleges Horizon requires pilots who report fatigue at their home base to travel to another city as passengers before taking the rest provided under their contract.

The union alleges the practice violates the Railway Labor Act and is asking the court to order Horizon to stop it and restore the negotiated rest protections.

“Management cannot pick and choose which parts of our contract it wants to follow,” said Sean Lenney, a Horizon pilot and member of Local 1224, in a news release.

The Teamsters also said Thursday that 99% of pilots who voted backed a no-confidence measure against Carlos Zendejas, Horizon’s senior vice president of operations. The union’s Horizon Air Pilots Executive Council called for his removal following the vote.

Horizon and its pilots have been negotiating a new collective bargaining agreement for more than three and a half years, according to the union. Pilots held informational pickets in Seattle and Boise, Idaho, on Oct. 15 over issues including pay, staffing, and training.

architecture that invites nature as a designer, from dissolving walls to insect-built pavilions

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

these built spaces invite natural forces into the design process, allowing materials to dissolve and buildings to change.

On South Korea’s Jeju Island, architecture studio stpmj built a small arch from rose-colored salt bricks, knowing that the island’s summer rainfall would eventually dissolve it. Over several months, the solid masonry gradually disappeared, leaving a delicate framework of mortar in its place. The weather had effectively been given permission to redesign the building.

Architecture typically seeks to control the natural forces around it, keeping water outside and protecting materials against decay. Yet a number of experimental structures propose a different relationship, allowing these conditions to become active participants in the design process.

From pavilions shaped by desert winds to structures constructed by insects, these seven projects explore what happens when architects give the environment a say in how their buildings take shape.

The walls of Dissolving Arch were always intended to disappear. Installed at the Osulloc Tea Museum on Jeju Island, the compact vault was built from translucent rock-salt bricks joined by cement mortar. Its pink masonry initially enclosed a narrow passageway, filtering daylight through the material before the island’s rainy season began to change its appearance.

The architects at stpmj deliberately chose materials with different reactions to water. While the salt bricks dissolved under repeated rainfall and humidity, the mortar remained, gradually revealing an open lattice where solid walls once stood. Over the exhibition period, the arch became more porous, admitting light and views of the surrounding landscape. Its final appearance emerged through the island’s weather, with rain completing a process that the architects had set in motion.

Amber-colored and almost glasslike, Neri Oxman‘s Aguahoja I rises five meters in a series of translucent surfaces that vary in thickness and flexibility. Developed by the designer together with the Mediated Matter Group at MIT, the pavilion was robotically fabricated from biopolymers derived from organic sources, including cellulose and chitosan. Its material composition allows it to respond to environmental exposure in ways conventional building materials are generally designed to resist.

Over time, the biopolymers can break down when exposed to water, allowing the structure to gradually return to the environment. When the original pavilion was later installed on an outdoor terrace at SFMOMA, researchers used instruments to monitor its exposure to the elements and study its decomposition. The project considers the eventual disappearance of architecture as part of its design, with the material’s lifespan considered from the beginning.

Winter temperatures became the principal building tool for Curtain, a temporary shelter installed on Winnipeg’s frozen Assiniboine River. Designed by Alejandro Felix and Fang Cui, the pavilion began with a simple timber framework wrapped in ropes. Water pumped from beneath the river ice was directed through a dripping system, allowing icicles to form along the suspended strands.

As the water continued freezing, ice accumulated into thick, irregular walls, gradually turning the open framework into an enclosed shelter. The process worked in reverse as temperatures rose. The icy surfaces thinned and became increasingly translucent before openings appeared and the timber structure emerged again.

Throughout the winter, the pavilion changed with the weather, its enclosure growing and disappearing according to the temperature outside.

Curtain, Alejandro Felix and Fang Cui, Winnipeg, Canada 2023Curtain, Alejandro Felix and Fang Cui, Winnipeg, Canada 2023

A bright-yellow steel ring now occupies the tidal Schuylkill River near Bartram’s Garden in Philadelphia. Known as FloatLab, the 75-foot-wide structure was developed by J. Meejin Yoon and Höweler + Yoon Architecture to rise and fall with changing water levels through a buoyancy and ballast system.

The river’s daily tidal cycle, which can produce fluctuations of approximately five to seven feet, determines the structure’s elevation. Its sloping circular walkway is designed to bring visitors toward the water’s surface while keeping them within the structure. FloatLab has arrived at its Philadelphia site, with public access planned for spring 2027.

Here, the architecture enters into a continuous relationship with a moving body of water, allowing the tides to govern its position throughout the day.

Strong desert winds supplied much of the final geometry for Ghost House, an installation by architecture studio i/thee in California’s Morongo Valley. The project began with two lightweight timber frames outlining the familiar shapes of small houses. Custom-cut canvas sheets, soaked in a water-based adhesive, were draped over the structures and left exposed to the mountain winds.

Over several hours, the fabric billowed and twisted before the adhesive hardened, fixing its windblown shapes in place. Square openings were subsequently cut into the surfaces, giving the sculptural forms the suggestion of windows.

The completed structures capture the atmospheric conditions of a particular moment, their irregular enclosures shaped by gusts moving through the site during construction. The architects established the framework, while the wind determined much of the finished geometry.

The bright-yellow canopies of Puddle Pavilion began as pools of liquid material spreading across the ground. For the installation beside Mud Creek in Bondurant, Iowa, architecture studio i/thee poured algae-based bio-resin directly onto a flat surface, allowing gravity and surface tension to influence its shape without conventional molds.

Once the resin had hardened, the irregular sheets were lifted onto slender steel columns, turning the flattened puddles into elevated architectural canopies. Their undulating edges and varying thicknesses preserve the behavior of the liquid during casting.

The architects controlled where the material was introduced, while its movement across the ground helped determine the finished geometry. What began as a flowing substance now hovers above the landscape, holding the shape it acquired before solidifying.

Some 17,532 silkworms participated in the construction of Silk Pavilion II, a six-meter-tall structure developed by Neri Oxman and the Mediated Matter Group. The insects were introduced onto a specially designed textile supported by a steel framework, where they spun layers of silk that became part of the architectural enclosure.

During fabrication, a rotating apparatus helped guide the silkworms across the surface, while changes in light and temperature influenced their movements and the density of the silk they produced. Their biological processes also affected the underlying fabric, creating openings within the finished enclosure.

Presented as part of a larger exhibition at New York’s Museum of Modern Art (see here), the pavilion combines human-designed infrastructure with a material surface physically produced by another species.

Across these seven experiments, architecture becomes something that can continue changing beyond the designer’s initial intervention. Rain gradually removes a wall, while freezing water constructs another. Elsewhere, the behavior of liquid materials or passing winds determines the shape of a structure before it is complete.

With Silk Pavilion II, the question extends to the construction process itself, where architects establish the conditions for another living organism to contribute to the finished work. The building becomes a collaboration, with its final form determined by more than the person who drew it.

This article is part of designboom’s POSTHUMAN UTOPIAS chapter, exploring what happens when humans are no longer the measure of all things. These stories rethink our relationship with the planet through interspecies architecture, living materials, regenerative landscapes and emerging technologies – learn more here! The chapter is presented in partnership with INDX|GLOBAL – a global cultural platform placing Indian architecture, design, and creative intelligence in conversation with the world.

Each designboom editorial series explores one theme in depth through projects, interviews, features and news across architecture, design, art and technology. Discover more chapters here, and get in touch if you’d like to submit a story through our submissions portal or contact form.

Dolly Parton’s Manager Dismisses Lawsuit Against Her Nephew

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Dolly Parton’s longtime manager has dropped his lawsuit alleging that the singer’s nephew, Bryan Seaver, posed a threat to her business empire after her death.

In a notice of voluntary dismissal filed Thursday, Danny Nozell and his lawyers said they were abandoning the claims without prejudice, meaning they could bring them again. The filing came shortly before Nozell’s deadline to respond to Seaver’s forceful denials of the allegations, and ahead of a hearing scheduled for Tuesday.

Lawyers for both sides did not immediately respond to Rolling Stone‘s requests for comment on the reason for the dismissal or whether the parties had reached a settlement.

At the hearing, a Nashville judge was expected to consider extending a prior restraining order that barred Seaver from coming within 1,000 feet of Parton’s employees and business partners, entering private property controlled by her posthumous company, She’s Alive LLC, or engaging in disruptive or harassing conduct.

The bitter legal feud spilled into public view in September when Nozell sued Seaver in Nashville Chancery Court, accusing him of waging an “obscene, deliberate, and escalating campaign of threats” intended to create “fear and chaos to extort money for his personal gain.” Nozell, who worked with and managed Parton for about two decades, is the sole manager of She’s Alive, a company that oversees the Dollywood theme park in Tennessee and the SongTeller hotel in Nashville, which houses a museum dedicated to Parton. Seaver, the son of Parton’s sister Cassie, vehemently denies threatening anyone.

The initial complaint, filed Sept. 22, cited a series of provocative messages sent by Seaver, which included statements such as, “I am about to become the hand of retribution for my entire family,” and “I’m not an entertainment person. I’m a killer.” In one message, Seaver allegedly threatened to launch a podcast aimed at damaging Parton’s brand partnerships, ending with the apparent demand: “Or fucking pay me.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Seaver forcefully disputed the allegations in court filings last month. He said his “pay me” remark referred to $251,000 he was owed for security services he had already provided to Parton and reflected his frustration with a bank’s new invoicing requirements. He further acknowledged that some of his private messages were profane, but he argued Nozell had taken them out of context to discredit him. Seaver said he was grieving Parton’s illness and death when he sent them, and he claimed that Nozell had encouraged some of his sentiments and even “hearted” a text in which Seaver said he was an arms dealer and mercenary. “Clearly, he wasn’t frightened or intimidated,” Seaver said of Nozell.

Seaver was the family member who announced Parton’s Aug. 25 death in a prerecorded video posted to her Instagram account that drew millions of reactions. According to court filings, he previously worked for private military contractors and provided security for Parton and her properties through his company, Squadron Augmented Protection Services.

In granting the initial temporary restraining order in September, Judge I’Ashea L. Myles said the messages cited in the complaint appeared to combine threats with demands for payment. In her ruling, she cited one message allegedly sent Aug. 19, about a week before Parton’s death at age 80. “I’m going to destroy the entire brand if you don’t get these [Dollywood business partners] out of our bit,” Seaver allegedly wrote.

In his response, Seaver included more context for the texts. In the same text where he mentioned he would “destroy the entire brand,” he went on to say, “I’ll earn you more money by virtue of their exit. Embarrassment on them at every turn is the answer. Let me keep at them. It costs you nothing. I am your ally. This is long game you need to start doing on them. The Dollywood brand is dying.” Related Content Dummymander: The GOP’s Bid to Rig the Midterm Map Failed Spectacularly Democrats Stopped Lecturing America — And Won Big Tim McGraw, Ella Langley, Kacey Musgraves to Perform at 2026 CMA Awards Russell Brand Found Guilty in Rape Trial

But the judge said the messages appeared to establish a “repeated course of conduct” in which Seaver allegedly coupled “threats with demands for payment or other action,” while invoking “his claimed access to weapons, experience with violence, and intention to retaliate.” Trending Stories Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’ Nicki Minaj Is Going Down With Trump’s Ship After Nearly Every Candidate She Backed Lost As Nicki Minaj Rallied for Trump's GOP, Her Lawyers Told Court She Still Owes Them $295,000 Bob Dylan Meets With Pope Leo XIV in a Private Audience

Seaver was removed from his security role with Parton’s business interests on Sept. 15, according to a letter filed in the case from attorneys for She’s Alive. The letter, obtained by Rolling Stone, made clear that his termination did not affect his separate status as a beneficiary of Parton’s trust.

“The co-trustees will be in contact with you and the other beneficiaries in the near future regarding your interest as a beneficiary of the Trust,” the letter stated. “All parties hope that this will be a smooth transition, and the trust group looks forward to corresponding with you as one of the beneficiaries.”

Palace’s ill-discipline showed ‘energy and fight’, says Sage

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Pierre Sage had no complaints about the number of yellow cards Crystal Palace were shown against Hoffenheim, highlighting it showed "energy and fight" from his players.

Sage was able to relieve some of the pressure surrounding his position at Selhurst Park with a 1-1 draw against Hoffenheim in the Europa League on Thursday.

The Eagles appeared set for a fourth defeat in five matches in all competitions when Alexander Prass' seventh-minute strike squirmed under the grasp of Dean Henderson.

And in Palace's quest for an equaliser, they thundered into challenges and saw Adam Wharton, Honest Ahanor, Yeremy Pino and Eddie Nketiah all booked in the space of seven minutes, and it got even worse for the hosts 13 minutes from time.

Wharton received his second yellow card for an elbow on Wouter Burger when challenging for the ball in the air. Indeed, no Premier League side have had more red cards in all competitions this season than Palace, with this their third (Manchester United and Sunderland also three).

But Daichi Kamada stepped up with a 91st-minute equaliser for Palace, though Sage does not want regular bookings to become a consistent occurrence for his team.

"For sure, we want to take fewer cards because we need all the players for all the competition," Sage told TNT Sports.

"With the energy and the fight, I think we were in a situation to take the risk of some cards.

"Now we just have to understand that we don't have to be in this situation by managing our game in a better way from the beginning."

On Wharton's red card, the Palace boss added: "I never comment on the decision of the referee. It's difficult to play without him.

"We suffered just after that, but we reorganised ourselves to play with a back four."

A point at Selhurst https://t.co/zDB1eQSDqn

— Crystal Palace F.C. (@CPFC) November 5, 2026

Since losing to AEK Larnaca in their first home match in major European competition, Palace are now unbeaten in their last eight at Selhurst Park (W5 D3).

But they once again struggled in front of goal on Thursday, with Kamada's goal, his fourth in his last 12 games for Palace, the Eagles' first in all competitions on home turf since a 3-1 win over Nottingham Forest on October 11.

Palace generated an expected goals (xG) total of just 1.05 from their 13 attempts, while they also had Henderson to thank for stopping Hoffenheim from going two goals ahead in the first half, with the Eagles captain producing a stunning stop to deny Andrej Kramaric.

"I think it could have been better if we scored before, but I know that Dean Henderson kept us in the game with his saves," Sage added.

"I like the energy of the team and to never give up until the end. This goal came at a good moment. We maybe had a last chance to win the game."

The pressure remains on Sage, though, and things do not get any easier for Palace as Andoni Iraola's Liverpool side make the trip to Selhurst Park this Sunday.

"First of all, recovery and we have to build the gameplan because it's a crazy team with really good players," Sage said of Palace's clash with Liverpool.

"They lost their last two games. I think they will come with a lot of ambition, and we have to deal with that."

10 Biggest Reveals From Final Fantasy 14's Tokyo Fanfest

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Stephanie Watel is a writer for DualShockers. She has 3 years of experience writing about video games, from news to lists to in-depth guides.

To be perfectly candid, Final Fantasy XIV’s Tokyo leg of the Fanfest circuit ahead of Evercold was a surreal and almost puzzling experience. Most of the expansion’s biggest surprises were already spoiled in both Anaheim and Berlin, while Tokyo essentially served as a clarity-and-elaboration session for much of what we already knew in a basic sense.

However, that isn’t to say that director Naoki (“Yoshi-P”) Yoshida’s keynote wasn’t full of impressively dense information for players to soak in. There was a lot to go around, to the point that Yoshi-P went almost 30 minutes past his allotted runtime for the presentation, on top of saying that it originally came out to almost four hours during rehearsal. That still doesn’t even include the “several” Producer Live Letter streams he also has planned leading up to Evercold’s launch to go over everything even further.

Square Enix veteran Koji Fox says lead writer Natsuko Ishikawa is snowed under and off-limits.

If anything, it’s a perfect metaphor for just how dramatically different Evercold is actually set to be, compared to all previous expansions in the game. Most of the game’s major content is getting a systemic overhaul, and those changes branch out even further, down to some of the smallest details. It’s almost on par with A Realm Reborn’s aptly named “rebirth” of the entire game over a decade ago, without having to shut the entire game down for nearly a year.

All that said, Tokyo Fanfest had plenty of information for players to take with them, and here are the biggest reveals from the entire keynote.

Evercold dropping in the dead of winter in January was poetically on point, and up through Berlin’s Fanfest we’ve known that Final Fantasy 14’s latest and most ambitious expansion yet was set to kick off a busy year for gamers (with the likes of Fable and Final Fantasy 7: Revelation also set for early Spring).

However, at the top of Yoshi-P’s keynote in Tokyo, he announced with regret that Evercold has gotten a three-week delay to late February. Now the expansion will debut on February 23, 2027, with a special Early Access period for those who pre-order starting on February 19.

As for why this is the case, Yoshi-P explained that, initially, Evercold would have been “barely” ready to go in time for their original January release deadline, which going by a three-week delay means that the expansion would have arrived at the tail end of month. However, due to “several issues” they encountered during development, Yoshi-P made the executive decision to impose the delay to ensure those problems are properly resolved before launch.

Despite this, the delay hasn’t impacted when players will get to preorder Evercold, and you can do so for all platforms starting November 4, 2026. Ultimately, given just how much is changing in the game systemically with Evercold, it’s just as well that this delay happened, and January did seem a bit ambitious already. Nonetheless, the rock isn’t getting kicked too far down the street, and the expansion will be in players’ hands soon enough.

On the heels of Evercold’s amended release date announcement, Yoshi-P pivoted straight to the grand reveal of the expansion’s second new combat job. Granted, there was strong speculation already after the many hints dropped at Berlin’s Fanfest that it revolved around something akin to a Hunter or a Falconer, and it was already confirmed that it would be a physical ranged DPS.

Finally, all the theory crafting was laid to rest with the reveal of Zephyr, courtesy of Visna posing as the job’s mascot. As shown in the active demonstration video, the player wields a crossbow weapon while flanked by a magical falcon familiar known as a Nephele. The video displayed various combat abilities, from basic attacks with the crossbow to more striking, magic-imbued ones that have your work together with your falcon to produce some spectacular moves.

What’s more, during a Q&A session on the second day of Tokyo’s Fanfest, Yoshi-P answered a fan’s question about being able to customize your bird companion. It turns out that you actually can thanks to a dual-dye system that allows you to recolor the bird when visiting a “certain NPC” in the game.

While FF14 players knew that changes to gear would be inevitable with Evercold’s new activity system, Yoshi-P surprised just about everyone during the keynote with sweeping alterations to raid gear in particular.

While Seasonal Gear is intended for “everyday” players with a simple optimization routine, Raid Gear comes with a few different new details. The first and most surprising change is that every single person in a raid group will now get their own personal chest drop after completing the duty, rather than having the group roll the dice on a single chest once a week. This will make raiding significantly more efficient (especially for premade groups or “statics”) in lieu of having to farm several runs just for the sake of gearing everyone up for the next raid.

Alongside that, players will also get to edit the “secondary attributes” of raid gear, allowing them to personally customize how their earned gear performs rather than just being stuck with preset attributes. Also, the item level of Raid Gear can be raised twice, and materia slots will unlock upon reaching that maximum item level.

Yoshi-P went into explicit detail in this section of the keynote to explain exactly how the gear upgrading process will work, and while it felt somewhat like a labyrinth of steps and stat numbers, it nonetheless looks like a promising move in the right direction for the game’s raid community in general.

The core that’s tying mostly everything together in Evercold’s revamped gameplay is the all-new Activity Level system. The Berlin keynote gave a soft introduction to this system with a basic explanation of weekly missions that will replace Final Fantasy 14’s traditional daily duties.

During the Tokyo keynote, Yoshi-P tied that back in with the introduction of the Activity Level system, which is how all players will obtain their Seasonal Gear throughout each “season” of the expansion’s patch cycle. As you complete Missions from the long list of available options, you’ll earn Activity Points that in turn raise your Activity Level. As that level goes up, you’ll gradually unlock rewards for your efforts, ranging from gil to emotes to mounts, and then so-called “gear categories”.

Unlocking these gear categories allows you to receive gear pieces of that category (e.g. - accessories) for your jobs. Furthermore, each category is shared across all jobs, so you don’t have to worry about your alt jobs missing out on those rewards. Redeeming those gear rewards is also done via the game’s new character UI dashboard.

Up to this point, the basic understanding that we have about our journey on the Fourth is that most of it will occur on the planet’s Fimm Himnar realmships, or the “Five Firmaments Left to Mankind”. These absolutely massive realmships each pertain to a different element and a certain sector of produced resources that can’t otherwise be obtained on the frozen surface.

Back in the Berlin Fanfest, Yoshi-P provided a detailed look at two of these realmships. One was Naglfar, the Realmship of Water where Visna resides. The other was Hringhorni, the Realmship of Fire where we now know that Bastion-wielding Baldr serves as its foreman.

Here's everything you need to know Final Fantasy XIV Evercold, from new characters, new factions, and how we're able to travel to different shards.

This time around, in-depth looks were provided for two more of these realmships. One was the Realmship of Earth, Sessrymnir, and a walkthrough video gave insight into the ship’s agriculture and mining production areas. Meanwhile, the Realmship of Wind, Skidbladnir, was also shown off in all its glory. Not only is it where the ridiculously adorable Kottr live, but it’s where they work on the production of airships, the main method of transportation on the Fourth.

The fifth and final realmship remains a mystery, which is likely for the sake of story spoilers, and it also remains to be seen what other explorable areas there may be. After all, a traditional Final Fantasy XIV expansion normally has at least six major zones that players can freely navigate, and the five realmships leave at least one of those zones in limbo. The closest guess would be somewhere on the subzero surface of the Fourth, but players will have to wait and see in February.

Alliance Raids aren’t just getting a new collaboration with Neon Genesis Evangelion for Evercold, they’re also getting their own systemic overhaul for the new expansion and beyond. These changes, according to Yoshi-P, reflect a few overarching issues that the content has suffered from over past expansions.

These issues start with skewed runtimes for the game’s entire roster of Alliance Raids. Some are generally more “efficient” to run, while others (e.g. - Tower of Paradigm’s Reach) are notoriously long and thus considered not optimal for dailies, leveling, farming gear, what have you. This gaping divide in runtime has since led to a bias within the player community, and leaves certain Alliance Raids infamously difficult to queue for, much less actually complete.

However, Yoshi-P has sought out a solution in the form of subdividing Alliance Raids into two instanced chunks. The first chunk will consist of the overall raid’s first two bosses (with trash mobs in between), while the second chunk will be the final two bosses in the same fashion. After completing the first half and obtaining your rewards from it, you’ll then be able to queue separately at your own discretion for the second half, complete it, and earn separate rewards from that chunk.

This is to help alleviate the burden of farming endlessly for certain gear items over the course of a 30 minute to 1 hour raid instance, all while also being able to enjoy the content more feasibly with less of a time commitment. Furthermore, Yoshi-P hinted at not having to roll anymore for loot from these raids, with a promise to elaborate more on that closer to Evercold’s launch.

Finally, Alliance Raids get one more big update starting in Evercold in the form of a legitimate “Savage” mode. Influenced by the success of Variant and Criterion Dungeon content, this will be an 8-player mode, rather than the usual lineup of 24 WoLs, and will function more as a boss rush-style instance with two bosses from the original raid set at “Savage Turn 3”-level difficulty. This mode will kick off with Evercold’s Evangelion collaboration raid series.

While many of the game’s existing combat modes are getting their own unique changes, there’s also an entirely new type of combat content debuting during Evercold called the Gauntlet of Power.

As its name suggests, it’s a gauntlet-style instance that functions as a more casual (compared to Alliance Raid Savage, at least) boss rush for players to embark on at their leisure. That is to say, it’s a mode that can be easily accessed from almost anywhere via the game’s overhauled UI. It can be played either with a premade group or a random queue, in much the same fashion as other instanced duties in the game.

This boss rush consists of up to five “floors” for a light party group, and clearing each floor leads to rewards that weren’t yet revealed during the keynote. Players can vote whether or not to continue the gauntlet, which provides a flexibility that not many of the other types of battle content in the game have. Despite being simple in concept, it may very well help bridge a gap that’s been lingering in Final Fantasy’s 14 gameplay flow for several years.

What’s more, this mode will help keep things interesting by choosing from an assortment of 200 different existing bosses in the game. This assures that the mode will traditionally prevalent issue of content monotony as much as possible while maintaining a sense of liberty and gratification for the player to make the most of their time.

Another big surprise that Yoshi-P was surprisingly candid about during Tokyo Fanfest’s Q&A session is that Allagan Tomestones of Poetics, the currency that’s remained in the game the longest (aside from maybe MGP), is set to officially retire in Evercold.

Yoshi-P did say that he shouldn’t really be discussing it just yet, but did confirm the plan nonetheless. He did offer a brief explanation in that “there will be something that allows you to trade for gear for alt jobs, or past rewards; you don’t have to worry about it for now. It just won’t be Poetics anymore.”

For players who have been in Final Fantasy 14 for at least some amount of time, Allagan Tomestones has since become the fall-back currency for the game’s more niche purchases. Alternatively, you could redeem them for one of the game’s more recent variants of tomestone currencies. Beyond A Realm Reborn, Poetics lose much of their remaining purpose, and at this point they feel more like a souvenir that just collects dust from one expansion to the next.

However, on that note, with the game’s dramatic revamp to the Activity Points system and Seasonal Gear, it remains to be seen if other tomestone currencies, such as those we had in Dawntrail, will keep some sort of utility themselves. Yoshi-P hasn’t been abundantly clear on just how long tomestone currency will hang on for, and if it will perhaps deviate to some other set purpose in the game. Otherwise, Evercold may be the “poetic” farewell to tomestone currency across the board as we know it.

While not necessarily anything to do with the game’s many different content systems, the keynote did get a small update on the story front in the form of identity reveals for certain characters. Baldr, the one wielding Bastion gear in the trailer, was revealed as the foreman of the Hringhorni realmship. Meanwhile, an entirely new face appeared in the final version of the trailer that ironically looked very familiar to many fans.

The presentation revealed her as Lagertha, the first mate of the Naglfar realmship where Visna resides. However, beyond finding out her purpose on the ship, fans are moreso scratching their heads over her striking resemblance to Ysayle, a key character who tragically passed during the events of Heavensward and the Dragonsong War.

However, players with a sharp eye may have caught the glimpse of her during the final Dawntrail MSQ content in Patch 7.56, meaning that her appearance is no coincidence. The most likely explanation for her presence on the Fourth is that Lagertha is her reflection. As to how deeply her character will impact the story of Evercold, players will simply have to wait and look forward to it.

On another non-combat tangent, Final Fantasy 14’s ever-controversial housing system is getting more bells and whistles added to it with Evercold’s patch cycle. Those changes are spread across “three pillars”, as indicated in the presentation.

First off, as hinted over the past several months, all houses will get an “interior area size customization” feature. This means that, for example, you can customize the interior of your small house to have the layout of a large house, for a currently undisclosed gil fee. Not only that, the furnishing limit will flex according to the layout you choose, so there’s no need to worry about getting cut off on your desired decor.

Secondly, floating interior furnishings will no longer be something only achievable through unconventional PC glitching controls. Along with an all-new placement interface, players will be able to move furnishings more freely on the vertical axis on both console and PC platforms.

The third and final pillar consists of adding even more housing plots, though Yoshi-P noted that they’re still discussing how exactly they want to achieve that, whether it’s through more, smaller areas, “hidden” areas, et cetera. While more plots feels moreso like another bandaid to a system that itself needs an overhaul, it’s at least nice that they’re doing their best to improve what’s in place until that change hopefully comes one day.

An updated version of the Xbox PC app available to Xbox Insiders loads up to 60% faster, and it's got some other nice improvements too.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Xbox has joined the 'faster launcher load times' arms race, announcing that its updated Xbox PC app, available now to Xbox Insiders, launches up to 60% faster, and has also been overhauled to offer "simpler navigation, updated filters, and new ways to organize your games."

Along with the faster launch time, Xbox said the home, store, and Game Pass pages in the updated app load up to 50% faster, while in-app windows open up to 65% faster—depending upon factors including device, internet connection, system configuration, and usage, of course. Library navigation has been simplified, search has been improved, and friends and social features are now part of the main navigational window.

Credit where it's due: That change to friends and social features is welcome. I don't use the Xbox App much—to be blunt, it's pretty much exclusively for Sea of Thieves. But after a long stretch away from it, I recently jumped back in for some high seas adventures with the crew, and I'll tell you this: Figuring out how to connect with my fellow online swashbucklers was kind of a headache compared to the super-simplicity of Steam. It worked fine, it just wasn't smooth and easy, so anything that helps with that, I'm happy to take.

I'm less excited about the faster loading times. Not that I'm going to complain about performance improvements, I'm just not sure it matters in the grand scheme of things. When the Epic Games Store promised that its "vibe coded" launcher was faster than Steam, a common reaction was mockery: Much of that was sparked by Epic's insistent banging of the 'AI is inevitable' drum, but there was also a lot of 'who cares?' in the reactions, too. I think the Xbox PC app may face the same: Faster is good, sure, but is it going to convince many PC gamers to use it instead of Steam when they don't have to? Of this, I have doubts.

(Steam also recently dropped a performance update that promises to improve loading times as well, blunting the impact of both the Epic and Xbox boosts even further.)

If you want to check out the updated app for yourself, you'll need to be in the Xbox Insiders program—for that, you'll need to install the Xbox Insider Hub. A "refreshed Xbox web experience" is also on the way, with an improved library tab, more efficient use of screen space, and light, dark, and high-contrast visual modes.

For all intents and purposes, Black Friday sales are already here. There are plenty of deals out already, even though we're weeks away from the actual event. Best Buy has announced the following events:

DoorBoosters: A Halloween sale from Oct. 30 to Nov. 1.

Early Black Friday Deals and Doorbusters: The official start of early Black Friday deals on Nov. 6. New deals will drop every Friday.

Black Friday Doorbusters and Deals: Best Buy says this will be its main sale, starting on Thursday, Nov. 19 and running until Nov. 28 with deals on "computing, gaming, home theater, wearables, and more."

Cyber Sunday and Monday: Best Buy is extending Cyber Monday to five days and says you can find deals going up to 50% off select tech.

Last-minute savings: The Christmas sale will run from Dec. 18 until Dec. 24.

There will be new deals every week in stores, on its website, and in the Best Buy mobile app. They'll offer price matching, meaning if the price drops further during the sale (Dec. 24), they will match it and offer a refund.
